People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I don’t talk much, and I feel kind of flat.\n\nEven at work, just getting started on my cleaning route feels… harder than it should. Like I’m pushing a loaded cart uphill, if that makes sense. I figured maybe I should talk to someone before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I mean… a little less than I used to, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything at all, but things I used to look forward to just don’t do much for me lately.\n\nLike Saturdays, I’d usually be thinking about fishing, or I’d sit with coffee and do my crosswords. Even at work, I used to like showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor right. Now I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, most days. I try not to sit around calling it “sad” all the time, because I can still get through what I need to, but it’s been hanging around a while.\n\nIt started not too long after I moved, and… if I’m being honest, it’s been about a year and a couple months now. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Some, I guess… but not like before. I used to really look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or just sitting with my coffee and doing crosswords. And at work, I used to enjoy showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor properly.\n\nLately I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me much back anymore. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but… yeah.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over a year, I think. About a year and two months.\n\nIt started not too long after I moved. I kept thinking it would pass once I got settled, you know, but it’s kind of stayed with me. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still know those things should be nice, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or doing crosswords with my coffee. Even at work, I liked showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor right. Lately I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back the way they used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there were times it was worse than this. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but when I was at my lowest, I was barely eating, and I felt guilty about everything. I’d have thoughts like maybe it wouldn’t be so bad if I didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… around that same time, at work, I was hearing that man’s voice again, and I really believed people in the building were watching me. I know that probably sounds bad. But yes, that was worse than how it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt similarly before this episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really like this, no.\n\nI mean, everybody has rough patches, and I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but I don’t remember it going on the same way as this. Not that I can clearly point to, anyway. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low or worse at any other time in your life before the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can honestly remember.\n\nI’ve had bad moods before, sure, and rough times like anybody, but nothing I’d call this low, or worse, before this past year. At least not in a way that stands out to me. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s usually there most of the day, I think.\n\nI mean, it can come up stronger or fade a little depending on what’s going on, but since the move, the sadness and low energy have kind of been in the background a lot. Even on days when I’m more caught up worrying about other things, it’s still there underneath.\n\nI don’t know if I’m explaining that right, but it hasn’t just been quick spells that pass.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years.\n\nIt’s been, I guess, about a year and five months now. It kind of started building after I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but that’s about when it began and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but when I was at my lowest, I was barely eating, and I felt guilty about everything. I’d have thoughts like maybe it wouldn’t be so bad if I didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… around that same time, at work, I was hearing that man’s voice again, and I really believed people in the building were watching me. I know that probably sounds bad. But yes, that was worse than how it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt similarly before this episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really like this, no.\n\nI mean, everybody has rough patches, and I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but I don’t remember it going on the same way as this. Not that I can clearly point to, anyway. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low or worse at any other time in your life before the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can honestly remember.\n\nI’ve had bad moods before, sure, and rough times like anybody, but nothing I’d call this low, or worse, before this past year. At least not in a way that stands out to me. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but that’s about when it began and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this low in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not like this.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but if you mean this same kind of low that sticks around and feels hard to shake… I’d say this is the one main time I can point to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past where you felt better, before this current episode of feeling low started?\n\nPatient: Before this started, yes… I think I was more okay. Not perfect or anything, but I was getting along better before I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood.\n\nSince then it’s been building, for about a year and five months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s really when I noticed things changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I haven’t really been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say a number I’m not sure about.\n\nBut most days food just doesn’t taste like much, kind of like cardboard, and I’ll skip meals or just have a few crackers after my shift. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I haven’t really been feeling like myself lately. It’s not anything dramatic, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ve gotten real quiet with people. I don’t talk much, and I feel kind of flat.\n\nEven at work, just getting started on my cleaning route feels… harder than it should. Like I’m pushing a loaded cart uphill, if that makes sense. I figured maybe I should talk to someone before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I mean… a little less than I used to, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t enjoy anything at all, but things I used to look forward to just don’t do much for me lately.\n\nLike Saturdays, I’d usually be thinking about fishing, or I’d sit with coffee and do my crosswords. Even at work, I used to like showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor right. Now I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, most days. I try not to sit around calling it “sad” all the time, because I can still get through what I need to, but it’s been hanging around a while.\n\nIt started not too long after I moved, and… if I’m being honest, it’s been about a year and a couple months now. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Some, I guess… but not like before. I used to really look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or just sitting with my coffee and doing crosswords. And at work, I used to enjoy showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor properly.\n\nLately I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me much back anymore. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but… yeah.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over a year, I think. About a year and two months.\n\nIt started not too long after I moved. I kept thinking it would pass once I got settled, you know, but it’s kind of stayed with me. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still know those things should be nice, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or doing crosswords with my coffee. Even at work, I liked showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor right. Lately I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back the way they used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there were times it was worse than this. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but when I was at my lowest, I was barely eating, and I felt guilty about everything. I’d have thoughts like maybe it wouldn’t be so bad if I didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… around that same time, at work, I was hearing that man’s voice again, and I really believed people in the building were watching me. I know that probably sounds bad. But yes, that was worse than how it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt similarly before this episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really like this, no.\n\nI mean, everybody has rough patches, and I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but I don’t remember it going on the same way as this. Not that I can clearly point to, anyway. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low or worse at any other time in your life before the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can honestly remember.\n\nI’ve had bad moods before, sure, and rough times like anybody, but nothing I’d call this low, or worse, before this past year. At least not in a way that stands out to me. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s usually there most of the day, I think.\n\nI mean, it can come up stronger or fade a little depending on what’s going on, but since the move, the sadness and low energy have kind of been in the background a lot. Even on days when I’m more caught up worrying about other things, it’s still there underneath.\n\nI don’t know if I’m explaining that right, but it hasn’t just been quick spells that pass.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years.\n\nIt’s been, I guess, about a year and five months now. It kind of started building after I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but that’s about when it began and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this low in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not like this.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but if you mean this same kind of low that sticks around and feels hard to shake… I’d say this is the one main time I can point to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past where you felt better, before this current episode of feeling low started?\n\nPatient: Before this started, yes… I think I was more okay. Not perfect or anything, but I was getting along better before I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood.\n\nSince then it’s been building, for about a year and five months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s really when I noticed things changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I haven’t really been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say a number I’m not sure about.\n\nBut most days food just doesn’t taste like much, kind of like cardboard, and I’ll skip meals or just have a few crackers after my shift. My work pants have been hanging looser than they used to, so… yeah, I guess I have lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing that man's voice or feeling like people are watching you at work?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, sometimes. Not all the time, but it still happens.\n\nWhen I’m mopping after hours, I’ll hear a man’s voice say my name, or say I missed a spot. It sounds really clear, like he’s standing over by the stairwell, but when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange… I’m not trying to make more of it than it is. It’s just what happens sometimes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to?\n\nPatient: Some, I guess… but not like before. I used to really look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or just sitting with my coffee and doing crosswords. And at work, I used to enjoy showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor properly.\n\nLately I mostly just let those things sit. They don’t really give me much back anymore. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it, but… yeah.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over a year, I think. About a year and two months.\n\nIt started not too long after I moved. I kept thinking it would pass once I got settled, you know, but it’s kind of stayed with me. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still know those things should be nice, if that makes sense.\n\nLike I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or doing crosswords with my coffee. Even at work, I liked showing the younger guys little tricks, like how to strip a floor right. Lately I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me anything back the way they used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there were times it was worse than this. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but when I was at my lowest, I was barely eating, and I felt guilty about everything. I’d have thoughts like maybe it wouldn’t be so bad if I didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… around that same time, at work, I was hearing that man’s voice again, and I really believed people in the building were watching me. I know that probably sounds bad. But yes, that was worse than how it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt similarly before this episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… not really like this, no.\n\nI mean, everybody has rough patches, and I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but I don’t remember it going on the same way as this. Not that I can clearly point to, anyway. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low or worse at any other time in your life before the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can honestly remember.\n\nI’ve had bad moods before, sure, and rough times like anybody, but nothing I’d call this low, or worse, before this past year. At least not in a way that stands out to me. Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s usually there most of the day, I think.\n\nI mean, it can come up stronger or fade a little depending on what’s going on, but since the move, the sadness and low energy have kind of been in the background a lot. Even on days when I’m more caught up worrying about other things, it’s still there underneath.\n\nI don’t know if I’m explaining that right, but it hasn’t just been quick spells that pass.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years now?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years.\n\nIt’s been, I guess, about a year and five months now. It kind of started building after I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but that’s about when it began and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this low in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not like this.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times where I was down or stressed, but if you mean this same kind of low that sticks around and feels hard to shake… I’d say this is the one main time I can point to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past where you felt better, before this current episode of feeling low started?\n\nPatient: Before this started, yes… I think I was more okay. Not perfect or anything, but I was getting along better before I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood.\n\nSince then it’s been building, for about a year and five months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but that’s really when I noticed things changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I haven’t really been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say a number I’m not sure about.\n\nBut most days food just doesn’t taste like much, kind of like cardboard, and I’ll skip meals or just have a few crackers after my shift. My work pants have been hanging looser than they used to, so… yeah, I guess I have lost some weight.\n\nDoctor: Are you still hearing that man's voice or feeling like people are watching you at work?\n\nPatient: I… yeah, sometimes. Not all the time, but it still happens.\n\nWhen I’m mopping after hours, I’ll hear a man’s voice say my name, or say I missed a spot. It sounds really clear, like he’s standing over by the stairwell, but when I look, nobody’s there.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange… I’m not trying to make more of it than it is. It’s just what happens sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of feeling this low before the one that started about a year and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like this, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had down times before, sure, but this depression part has really been there for about a year and two months, starting not long after the move. That sounds about right, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other episodes of depression like this one before the past year?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had another one like this before.\n\nI mean, I’ve had normal rough patches, like anyone, but this depression part has really been with me for about a year and two months now, starting not long after the move. That’s the best way I can put it, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes of depression before the one that started about a year and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had hard stretches before, but nothing I’d call the same as this.\n\nThe depression part has really been with me for about a year and two months, starting not long after the move. That’s how I remember it, anyway… if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Has your current episode of feeling low lasted for two years or more?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s been two years. It’s been more like… about a year and two months, I guess, starting not too long after the move. So not two years, if I’m understanding right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
